# Paired statistics v5 — paired statistics

Status: **PARTIAL / direction decomposition BLOCKED by missing original per-cell predictions**. The paired seed analyses, pooled-gap bootstraps and historical Holm families are computed. No new fitting or model inference was performed. Tables 14/15 and their tests remain post-hoc.

The exact machine-readable values, all four seed observations, source file/field for every observation, and SHA-256 input catalog are in `paired_stats_v5.json`. Markdown displays nine significant digits; JSON retains binary64 values. `Pxxxx` identifies an exact JSON `paired_contrasts` row. Source cells below show seed 0; the JSON supplies the corresponding seed 1–3 fields and all hashes.

## Definitions and scope

A paired difference is the left score minus the right score at the same recorded training seed (0–3). Negative RMSE/NRMSE differences favor the left model; positive direction-accuracy differences favor the left model. The paired sample standard deviation is computed from the four differences, with divisor 3. The 95% paired t confidence interval (CI) is mean ± t(0.975,3) × paired SD / 2; p-values are two-sided. They rely on the model of independent, normally distributed seed differences. Simulation seeds also change the patient split. Clinical seeds share fixed cohorts, so seed inference conditions on those cohorts and does not establish population or hospital generalization.

Minimum detectable difference (MDD) is a plug-in sensitivity calculation: for n=4 pairs, alpha=0.05 (two-sided), 80% power, the exact noncentral-t inversion gives MDD = 2.12794934 × paired SD (noncentrality 4.25589869). It treats the observed paired SD as a planning population SD; with four pairs this estimate is uncertain. It is not observed power, an equivalence margin or evidence that a null effect is absent. Every ordinary null row has an MDD. Identity references have zero variance and no inferential test/MDD (NA).

The seed t tests below are newly computed, exploratory and unadjusted. The historical Holm families are the stay-level Wilcoxon families explicitly reconstructed later; they do not adjust this new collection of seed t tests. A raw CI excluding zero is therefore not a familywise significance claim. Extra organ/model comparisons are included to make all stated orderings inspectable. Comparisons across corpora/designs preserve common seed labels but change fitted models or populations as stated.

Reviewer check (P0189): mono-matched minus none at kappa=1 is positive in 4/4 seeds; mean 0.00249458325, paired SD 0.000793709146, 95% CI [0.00123161488, 0.00375755162], exact two-sided paired-t p = `0.008131157406764473`. This contradicts the paper's blanket inference from marginal seed SD or from there being only four seeds.

## Stay-level paired bootstrap of pooled gaps versus GRU-D

Each of 2,000 draws samples n evaluable stays with replacement, using the same indices for both fitted models. For each draw and each model, pooled RMSE = sqrt(sum saved stay SSE / sum saved observed-target count); the gap is the difference of these two roots. SSE means sum of squared errors. The denominator varies with the resample and counts repeated stays repeatedly. Zero-target stays contribute no pooled error/count and are excluded from resampling. Resampling them as eligible zero-contribution records defines a different finite-sample bootstrap; the exclusion here matches the paper's evaluable-stay convention. The conditional unit is a stay, not a target entry, seed, or person. The saved SSE arrays use float32 per-stay reductions; totals here use float64, so the array-derived point and frozen run-JSON point can differ slightly. Both exact points and their difference are retained in JSON. Percentile CIs use NumPy linear quantiles; RNG is default_rng(0) for each seed/split.

Coverage is the eight non-GRU-D fitted models with saved per-stay error arrays under results/v4/selected. Persistence and the two primary learned-graph adapters have no such arrays in that selected series, so no pooled stay-bootstrap interval is inferred from their scalar RMSE records. Their saved scalar seed contrasts are included above.

## Separate mean-stay RMSE bootstrap versus GRU-D

These CIs use the same resampled stays, but average the within-stay square roots before differencing. Their denominator is the number of evaluable sampled stays, giving each stay equal weight. They are not pooled-gap intervals. All source paths and excluded counts are the same as the corresponding B_ID in the preceding table; source fields are sse/count followed by the within-stay square root.

## Explicit historical Holm families for section 7.1

There are eight separate families (four checkpoints × two splits), each with nine members. Every member compares PhysioGraph-GRU with one of: persistence, GRU, GRU-med, GRU-large, GRU-D, Neural CDE, ODE-RNN, GraphGRU, PhysioGraph-SSM. Neural CDE B was included in the historical numerical family even though it is excluded from primary model ranking; it is retained here to reproduce that family rather than silently redefine it. The tests are two-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank tests on paired stay RMSE differences. For ordered p(1) ≤ ... ≤ p(9), adjusted p(i) = min(1, max_{j≤i} (10−j)p(j)). The family contains neither the two learned graphs nor graph variants, horizons, simulator contrasts or the new seed t tests.

All 72 adjusted p-values reproduce the saved values. For the GRU-D member, all eight Holm-adjusted values are below 1e-35. These tests have no paired t CI or n=4 MDD, because their sampling unit is a stay; the separate seed t table answers the four-run question.

## Direction accuracy: identifiable counts and missing information

The original h5 aggregate has 108 four-seed cells: 96 shared-history and 12 historical legacy cells. Each run has 120 test patients × 4 adjacent dose pairs × 24 target samples × 6 channels = 69,120 cells. Removing the first target leaves 66,240 cells. Shared-history exact-zero truth cells number 2,400 (all at the first target in five channels); CVP has a direct nonzero effect at that target. Legacy rows are historical diagnostics and do not isolate a shared-versus-legacy protocol effect because sigma differs. Counts below are computed independently from saved target arrays at the original recorded test indices. Sign numerators and predicted-zero marginals come from the original per-run direction records and reproduce the h5 aggregate. **Bounds are not point estimates or CIs.**

These direction rows are descriptive counts and partial-identification bounds, so paired t exclusions and MDD do not apply to this table. Zero-effect accuracy is exactly zero whenever the saved predicted-zero count is zero; otherwise the intersection of true-zero and predicted-zero cells is not saved and its rigorous bounds are retained per seed. An all-cell accuracy does not identify accuracy after the first target is removed: correct first-target CVP cells are unknown. Similarly, thresholded true-effect counts can be computed from targets but correctness on them requires predictions. The JSON therefore gives per-seed denominators for exploratory tolerances 1e-6, 1e-4 and 1e-3 times each channel's range in the saved reference archive (and excludes the first target). Thresholds retain channel units; a cell is eligible only if its absolute true adjacent-dose effect exceeds that threshold. All corresponding accuracy fields are null. No threshold was specified in the card; these explicit sensitivity definitions do not claim to be historical choices.

## Effect-tolerance denominators from saved target arrays

Each entry lists the four per-seed counts in seed order 0,1,2,3. First-target cells are excluded; only true effects exceeding the channel-specific threshold are eligible. Numerators/accuracies remain unavailable. For every count and physical threshold, the JSON field is `direction_decomposition[cell].per_seed[seed].effect_tolerance_variants[tolerance]`; its truth_source_file/field and range_source_file/field identify the saved arrays exactly.

This table reports eligible true-effect counts, not a direction-recovery result or a null contrast. No t CI or MDD applies. Exact accuracy and its paired comparisons remain blocked by missing original predictions. The saved verifier file `BASE_SIM-verify/artifacts/h5_shared_predictions.npz` was inspected and has only four test patients; it is a smoke run and cannot replace any full-grid run with 120 test patients.

## Delivery limitations and handoff

The exact first-target-excluded and effect-tolerance direction scores require the original per-cell predicted trajectories (or sufficiently detailed sign/effect contingency arrays). Those are absent from the original H5 run tree and aggregate; its saved NPZs are generated truth/factual datasets. No model was retrained or reinferred. This evidence gap prevents full acceptance of item 4. The computation is an author/engineer artifact, not an independent scientific review. The project lead should replace marginal-SD/no-significance language with the paired results, preserving the exploratory status and multiple-comparison caveats. Current owner final-kit/outside-review workflow applies; the withdrawn automatic editorial passes and style gates were not reinstated. This is an evidence subtask, not a final paper kit, so prose_metrics is left to the project lead's once-per-final-kit invocation.
